Since we consider the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation, the classical DiPerna Lions theory based on Grad’s angular cutoff assumption cannot be applied. Specifically, we cannot follow the approach in
[
11
]
to obtain local integrability of the gain and loss terms of the collision operator by using Lemmas
2.10
-
2.11
(averaged velocity lemma) and the Arkeryd-type inequality.

To overcome this difficulty, we adopt the renormalization method introduced by Alexandre and Villani in Section 3 of
[
6
]
. In this approach, the renormalized collision term
\beta^{\prime}_{\delta}(f)Q(f,f)
is decomposed into three parts, denoted by
(\mathcal{R}_{1})
,
(\mathcal{R}_{2})
and
(\mathcal{R}_{3})
. The main strategy is to first show that
(\mathcal{R}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{R}_{2})
are well defined as distributions in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Then, by using the renormalized Boltzmann equation together with the non-negativity of these terms, we derive suitable bounds for
(\mathcal{R}_{3})
, which allows us to give a rigorous meaning to the renormalized collision operator.

4.1.
Cancellation lemma

The main purpose of this section is to analyze the integral term
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(f^{\prime}_{*}-f_{*})\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma
.
Since the collision kernel possesses a singularity at the angle
\theta=0
, we cannot directly show that this integral is finite. However, we observe that
v^{\prime}_{*}\simeq v_{*}
holds when the angle
\theta
is sufficiently close to
0
. This observation leads us to expect that the integral is still convergent. The purpose of the Cancellation lemma is to justify this fact.
The idea of the Cancellation lemma first appeared in the work of Villani
[
50
]
. It was later further developed in
[
5
,
6
]
. In this work, we mainly follow the formulation given in
[
6
]
.

Lemma 4.1

(Cancellation lemma,
[
6
]
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
. For the collision kernel
B
and for almost everywhere
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we have the following conclusion:

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}f\equiv\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\,B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(f^{\prime}_{*}-f_{*})dv_{*}d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}S(|v-v_{*}|)dv_{*},

where the kernel
S
is given by

(4.6)

\displaystyle S(|z|)=2\pi\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin\theta\left[\frac{1}{\cos^{3}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}B\left(\frac{|z|}{\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)},\cos\theta\right)-B(|z|,\cos\theta)\right]d\theta.

In particular,
S
is a locally integrable function satisfying the estimate

\displaystyle|S(|z|)|\leq\frac{1}{\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{8}\right)}\left[3M(|z|)+|z|M^{\prime}(|z|)\right].

Proof.

The proof is almost the same as Proposition 3.1 of
[
6
]
. We omit the details here for
simplicity.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\left(1+|v|^{2}\right)dxdv<+\infty.

If
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, then it follows that
(\mathcal{R}_{1})\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)\right)\right),
for any
R>0.

Proof.

We first recall the definition of
\beta_{\delta}(s)
in (
4.1
), which implies that both
f\beta^{\prime}_{\delta}(f)
and
\beta_{\delta}(f)
belong to
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))
for any fixed
\delta>0
. Thus, to complete the proof of the lemma, it suffices to show that

f*_{v}S\in L^{\infty}\big([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))\big).

Next, using the expression for the collision kernel
B
given in (
1.7
), we observe that there exists a constant
C_{\nu}
such that

S(z)\leq C\big(M(|z|)+|z|M^{\prime}(|z|)\big)\leq C_{\nu}|z|^{\gamma}.

If
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|f*S\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}f(t,x,v_{*})|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}f(t,x,v_{*})(|v-v_{*}|^{2}+1)dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,R}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v_{*})(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dxdv_{*}.

If
-3<\gamma<0
, one has

\displaystyle\|f*S\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times B_{R}(v)}f(t,x,v_{*})|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v_{*})dxdv_{*}\int_{B_{2R+1}(z)}|z|^{\gamma}dz+C_{\nu,R}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f(t,x,v_{*})dxdv_{*}.

Based on the local integrability of the term
|z|^{\gamma}
with
\gamma\in(-3,0)
, we conclude that

f*_{v}S\in L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)\right)
, which means
(\mathcal{R}_{1})\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)\right)\right)
for all
\gamma\in(-3,1]
.
∎

4.2.
The integrability of
(\mathcal{R}_{2})

When dealing with the key bilinear term
(\mathcal{R}_{2})
, our method differs from that presented in Section 3.2 of
[
6
]
. In their work, Alexandre and Villani used a second-order Taylor expansion to control the strong angular singularity and obtained boundedness of an operator from
W^{2,\infty}
to
L^{\infty}
in a dual sense. As a result, the upper bound in inequality (
4.9
) reduces to
\frac{1}{2}|v-v_{*}|\left(1+\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{2}\right)M(|v-v_{*}|)
. However, for hard potentials (
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
), the term
|v-v_{*}|^{2}M(|v-v_{*}|)
cannot be controlled by the available a priori bounds on mass and energy, i.e.,

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(1+|x|^{2}+|v|^{2})dxdv.

In fact, we have verified that a first-order Taylor expansion is sufficient to control the singularity induced by the collision kernel. In this case, choosing test functions

\varphi\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};W^{1,\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\right)

ensures that the term
(\mathcal{R}_{2})
is well-defined. Here, the space
W^{1,\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
is defined as the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
in
W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. However, in the critical case
\gamma=1
, the linear term
|v-v_{*}|
arising from the Taylor expansion couples with the factor
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}
in the collision kernel, leading to the breakdown of convergence for the renormalized operator
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
. A detailed analysis of this issue is provided in Section
6.2
.

To solve this difficulty, we use test functions from the Hölder space
C_{c}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
instead of
W^{1,\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. We set the parameter
\alpha
between
\nu
and
1
, and the detailed derivation is given in Lemma
4.4
. Although this change relaxes the regularity requirements for the test functions, it actually leads to a more regular dual space from the perspective of duality. Specifically, the dual space
B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}
is more regular than
(W^{1,\infty}_{0})^{*}
because the index
-\alpha
is strictly larger than
-1
when
\alpha<1
. This more refined estimate effectively compensates for the lack of Lipschitz continuity and ensures that the operator converges effectively even in the critical case.

To this end, let
\varphi(v)
be a test function depending only on the velocity variable. Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{2})\varphi(v)dv

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B\left[f^{\prime}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{\prime})-f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)\right]\varphi(v)dvdv_{*}d\sigma\,

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)dvdv_{*}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)d\sigma.

For a fixed
v_{*}
, we define the operator
\mathcal{T}
as

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\colon\varphi\longmapsto\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)\,d\sigma.

We shall show that
\mathcal{T}
is essentially bounded from
C_{c}^{\alpha}
to
L^{\infty}_{loc}
for
\alpha\in(\nu,1)
, which differs from Proposition 3.3 of
[
6
]
.

Lemma 4.3

(
C_{c}^{\alpha}\to L^{\infty}_{loc}
bound for
\mathcal{T}
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
and
\alpha\in(\nu,1)
with
\nu
defined in (
1.8
). Then for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})
,

(4.8)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}\varphi(v)|\leq C_{\nu,\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}\leq C^{\prime}_{\nu,\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}M(|v-v_{*}|)

for some constant
C_{\nu,\alpha},C^{\prime}_{\nu,\alpha}>0
.

Proof.

In view of the definition (
1.7
) of the collision kernel
B
and the range of parameters, we observe that for
\nu<\alpha<1
, the integral satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{\alpha}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin\theta d\theta<+\infty.

Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}(1-k\cdot\sigma)=|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right).

Consequently, we obtain the estimate

(4.9)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)|v^{\prime}-v|^{\alpha}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)\sin^{\alpha}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin\theta d\theta

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}.

This implies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}\varphi(v)|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)d\sigma\right|\leq C_{\nu,\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{C_{c}^{\alpha}}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}.

Together with (
1.16
), the proof of Lemma
4.3
is completed.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

If
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, then it follows that
(\mathcal{R}_{2})\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}(B_{R}(v))\right)\right)
, for any
R>0
and any
\nu<\alpha<1
.

Proof.

First, by the definition of the dual norm, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{R}_{2})\|_{B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}(B_{R}(v))}=

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{2})\varphi\,dv\;\Bigg|\;\varphi\in C^{\alpha}_{c}(B_{R}(v)),\,\|\varphi\|_{C^{\alpha}_{c}(B_{R}(v))}\leq 1\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}\beta_{\delta}(f)f_{*}|\mathcal{T}\varphi|dvdv_{*},

where
\alpha\in(\nu,1)
. In the case where
\gamma\in[-1,1]
, Lemma
4.3
yields

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{R}_{2})\|_{B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}(B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}\beta_{\delta}(f)f_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}\beta_{\delta}(f)f_{*}(1+|v-v_{*}|^{2})dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha,R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dv_{*}.

On the other hand, if
\gamma\in(-3,-1)
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{R}_{2})\|_{B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}(B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v)}\beta_{\delta}(f)f_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}dv_{*}\int_{B_{2R+1}(z)}|z|^{\gamma+1}dz+C_{\nu,\alpha,R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}dv_{*}.

In conclusion,
(\mathcal{R}_{2})\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T];L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};B^{-\alpha}_{1,1}(B_{R}(v))\right)\right)
for all
\gamma\in(-3,1]
.
∎

4.3.
The integrability of
(\mathcal{R}_{3})

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume that
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(t,x,v)\left(1+|v|^{2}\right)dxdv<+\infty.

If
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, then it follows that
(\mathcal{R}_{3})\in L^{1}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)\right)
, for any
R>0.

Proof.

This lemma essentially serves as an a priori estimate, based on which we regard the function
f
as a renormalized solution of the Boltzmann equation (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
1.1
. Multiplying both sides of the following equation by a test function
\varphi
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\beta_{\delta}(f)}{\partial t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\beta_{\delta}(f)=\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f)Q(f,f),

and integrating over
t,x,v
, we obtain

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\beta_{\delta}(f)(T,\cdot)\varphi dxdv=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\beta_{\delta}(f)(0,\cdot)\varphi dxdv

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left((\mathcal{R}_{1})+(\mathcal{R}_{2})+(\mathcal{R}_{3})\right)\varphi dxdv.

Here, the test function
\varphi
satisfies

\displaystyle\varphi(v)\geq 0,\quad\varphi\equiv 1\ \text{on}\ B_{R}(v),\quad\varphi\equiv 0\ \text{on}\ \mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\setminus B_{2R}(v),\quad\|\varphi\|_{C^{\alpha}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq C.

Consequently,

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{3})\varphi dxdv\leq

\displaystyle\left|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\beta_{\delta}(f)(T,\cdot)+\beta_{\delta}(f)(0,\cdot))\varphi dxdv\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}((\mathcal{R}_{1})+(\mathcal{R}_{2}))\varphi dxdv\right|.

Given that
\beta_{\delta}(f)=f/(1+\delta f)\leq C_{\delta}f
, we have

\left|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(\beta_{\delta}(f)(T,\cdot)-\beta_{\delta}(f)(0,\cdot)\right)dxdv\right|\leq C_{\delta}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Lemmas
4.2
and
4.4
ensure that the second term on the right-hand side of inequality (
4.10
) is finite.
Moreover, by the non-negativity of
(\mathcal{R}_{3})
, we conclude that

(\mathcal{R}_{3})\in L^{1}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)\right)\,.

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.5
.
∎

5.
Strong compactness of approximated solution

Based on the uniform bounds (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) of
f^{n}
and the Dunford-Pettis theorem (Lemma
2.1
), it follows directly that
f^{n}
converges weakly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. This section is devoted to upgrading the weak convergence results to strong convergence in
L^{1}
, specifically showing that
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. We first present several lemmas to establish an inequality which demonstrates that the high-frequency components of
\sqrt{f^{n}}
in the velocity variable are controlled by the entropy dissipation. By combining the weak compactness of
f^{n}
, the uniform bound (
3.11
) and the Vitali Convergence Theorem (Lemma
2.2
), we prove that
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. We note that this method yields strong convergence results only in the hard potential case. For soft potentials, We illustrate this limitation through a concrete counterexample (see Example
9.1
in Section
9
), where we construct a specific distribution function that satisfies finite entropy and moment bounds but results in an infinite collision frequency.

5.1.
A functional inequality

In this subsection, we establish a functional inequality for the non-cutoff model, demonstrating that the regularity of the distribution function with respect to the velocity variable is controlled by the entropy dissipation. This result is adapted primarily from
[
5
]
.

Let

(5.1)

\displaystyle e^{n}(t,x,v)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}(f^{n}f^{n}_{*}-{f^{n}}^{\prime}f_{*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}})\log f^{n}_{*}\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

and

(5.2)

\displaystyle Z_{n}(a)=2\pi\int_{a}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\sin\theta\,d\theta,

where
b_{n}
is defined in (
3.3
).

We now proceed to prove the following inequality

(5.3)

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}(t,x,v)dv+\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f^{n}_{*}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right\}.

Here,
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform with respect to the velocity variable
v
, and
\chi_{R}
is a smooth cutoff function satisfying
0\leq\chi_{R}\leq 1
with
\chi_{R}\equiv 1
on
B_{R}(v)
and
\supp(\chi_{R})\subset B_{R+1}(v)
. Furthermore, the term
C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}
is a constant, and
r_{0},R>0
are sufficiently small and large constants, respectively. The proof is detailed in the following series of lemmas.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
\|f\|_{L^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f(v)(1+|v|)dv>0
. Then for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that if
|\xi|\geq 1
, we have

(5.4)

\displaystyle Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)\leq C_{0}\|f\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma,

where
\xi^{-}=\frac{\xi-|\xi|\sigma}{2}
,
b_{n}
is defined in (
3.3
), and the constant
C_{0}>0
is independent of
n
and
f
.

Remark 5.1
.

This lemma is primarily based on Section 6 of
[
5
]
. However, the constant
C_{0}
on the right-hand side of inequality (
5.4
) originally depends on
\|f\|_{L^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}
and
\|f\|_{L\log L}
. Here, we refine the constant by factoring out the term
\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}
, so that
C_{0}
becomes independent of both
n
and
f
.

Proof.

From the definition of
\xi^{-}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\xi^{-}\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(|\xi|^{2}-|\xi|\xi\cdot\sigma\right)=\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{2}\left(1-\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right).

By using the half-angle identity
1-\cos\theta=2\sin^{2}\left(\theta/2\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(|\xi^{-}|^{2}\wedge 1\right)d\sigma=2\pi\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\left[|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\wedge 1\right]\sin\theta d\theta,

where we denote
a\wedge b=\min\{a,b\}
. Since
\sin x\geq\frac{2}{\pi}x
for all
x\in\left[0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right]
, we have
|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\geq|\xi|^{2}\theta^{2}/\pi^{2}
. Consequently, the following inequality holds

\left[|\xi|^{2}\theta^{2}\wedge 1\right]\leq\pi^{2}\left[|\xi|^{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\wedge 1\right].

This implies that for
|\xi|\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)=2\pi\int_{\frac{1}{|\xi|}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\sin\theta\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq 2\pi\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\sin\theta(|\xi|^{2}\theta^{2}\wedge 1)d\theta

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(|\xi^{-}|^{2}\wedge 1\right)d\sigma.

Next, we prove that for any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\zeta}
, the following bound holds

\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\zeta)|\geq C_{f}^{\prime}\,(|\zeta|^{2}\wedge 1).

We first observe that

\displaystyle\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\zeta)|=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta}}f(\eta)(1-\cos(\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha))\,d\eta=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta}}f(\eta)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha}{2}\right)\,d\eta,

where
\hat{f}(\zeta)=|\hat{f}(\zeta)|e^{-i\alpha},
with
\alpha\in(-\pi,\pi]
. Furthermore, whenever
|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|\geq 2\epsilon
for any
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, the inequality
\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha}{2}\right)\geq\sin^{2}\epsilon
holds. Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\zeta)|\geq 2\sin^{2}\epsilon\int_{\{|\eta|\leq r,\forall p\in\mathbb{Z},|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|\geq 2\epsilon\}}f(\eta)\,d\eta.

By considering the decomposition of the space

\displaystyle\left\{|\eta|\leq r,\forall p\in\mathbb{Z},|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|\geq 2\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{|\eta|\leq r,\exists p\in\mathbb{Z},|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|<2\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{|\eta|\geq r\right\}=\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta}

and the elementary tail estimate

\displaystyle\int_{|\eta|\geq r}|f(\eta)|d\eta\leq\frac{1}{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta}}|f(\eta)||\eta|d\eta,

we derive the lower bound

\displaystyle\int_{\{|\eta|\leq r,\forall p\in\mathbb{Z},|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|\geq 2\epsilon\}}f(\eta)d\eta\geq\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}-\frac{\|f\|_{L^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}}{r}-\int_{\{|\eta|\leq r,\exists p\in\mathbb{Z},|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi\leq 2\epsilon\}}f(\eta)d\eta.

We observe that for a fixed
p
, the condition

\displaystyle\left|\eta\cdot\frac{\zeta}{|\zeta|}+\frac{\alpha}{|\zeta|}-p\cdot\frac{2\pi}{|\zeta|}\right|\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{|\zeta|}

defines a thin slab region with width
\frac{4\epsilon}{|\zeta|}
and cross-sectional area
4r^{2}
.
Next, we examine the range of
p
. Given that
|\eta\cdot\zeta+\alpha-2p\pi|\leq 2\epsilon
and
|\eta\cdot\zeta|\leq|\eta|\cdot|\zeta|\leq r|\zeta|
, it follows that
|2p\pi-\alpha|\leq 2\epsilon+r|\zeta|
. This inequality can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-2\epsilon-r|\zeta|+\alpha\leq 2\pi p\leq 2\epsilon+r|\zeta|+\alpha,

which implies

\displaystyle p\in\left[\frac{\alpha-2\epsilon}{2\pi}-\frac{r|\zeta|}{2\pi},\frac{r|\zeta|}{2\pi}+\frac{\alpha+2\epsilon}{2\pi}\right]\cap\mathbb{Z}.

For sufficiently small
\epsilon
, we have
|\alpha\pm 2\epsilon|\leq\pi
. This implies that the number of such integer values
p
is about
\frac{r|\zeta|}{\pi}+1
. Therefore, the total volume of the integration region

\displaystyle A=\left\{|\eta|\leq r,\exists p\in\mathbb{Z},\left|\eta\cdot\frac{\zeta}{|\zeta|}+\frac{\alpha}{|\zeta|}-p\cdot\frac{2\pi}{|\zeta|}\right|\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{|\zeta|}\right\}

does not exceed that of region
\frac{16\epsilon}{|\zeta|}r^{2}\left(\frac{r|\zeta|}{\pi}+1\right)
.

Next, we discuss the value of the constant
C_{f}
for the two cases where
|\zeta|\geq 1
and
|\zeta|\leq 1
, respectively.

Case 1.
|\zeta|\geq 1
.
We note that the volume of the region
A
satisfies

\displaystyle|A|\leq\frac{16\epsilon}{|\zeta|}r^{2}\left(\frac{r|\zeta|}{\pi}+1\right)\leq 16\epsilon r^{2}+\frac{16\epsilon}{\pi}r^{3}.

Let the constant
C^{\prime}_{f}
be defined as

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{f}=2\sin^{2}\epsilon\left\{\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}-\frac{\|f\|_{L^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}}{r}-\sup_{|A|\leq 16\epsilon r^{2}+\frac{16\epsilon}{\pi}r^{3}}\int_{A}f(\eta)\,d\eta\right\}.

By choosing appropriate values of
\epsilon
and
r
, we can ensure that
C^{\prime}_{f}\geq\sin^{2}\epsilon\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}
.

Case 2.
|\zeta|\leq 1
.
Let
\delta=\frac{\epsilon}{|\zeta|}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\epsilon=\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta|\zeta|)}{\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}}\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}\geq\inf_{|\zeta|\leq 1}\left|\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta|\zeta|)}{\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}}\right|\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}.

Furthermore, we set

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{f}=2\delta^{2}\inf_{|\zeta|\leq 1}\left|\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta|\zeta|)}{\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}}\right|\times\left\{\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}-\frac{\|f\|_{L^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}}{r}-\sup_{|A|\leq 16\delta r^{2}\left(1+\frac{r}{\pi}\right)}\int_{A}f(\eta)\,d\eta\right\}.

Similarly, we can choose appropriate values of
\delta
and
r
such that

C^{\prime}_{f}\geq\delta^{2}\inf\limits_{|\zeta|\leq 1}\left|\frac{\sin^{2}(\delta|\zeta|)}{\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}}\right|\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}\,.

Consequently, there exists a positive constant
C_{0}
, independent of
f
, such that

\displaystyle|\zeta|^{2}\wedge 1\leq C_{0}\|f\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\zeta)|\right),\quad\forall\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\zeta}.

In particular, we have
|\xi^{-}|^{2}\wedge 1\leq C_{0}\|f\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)
. It follows that if
|\xi|\geq 1
,

\displaystyle Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(|\xi^{-}|^{2}\wedge 1\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|f\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\eta})}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

For any functions
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}),F\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(k\cdot\sigma)f_{*}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}}|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma\right\}d\xi,

where

\displaystyle\xi^{+}=\frac{\xi+|\xi|\sigma}{2},\quad\xi^{-}=\frac{\xi-|\xi|\sigma}{2}.

Proof.

First, we recall the Plancherel-type identity established in Property 1, Section 5 of
[
5
]
, which is expressed as

(5.5)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(k\cdot\sigma)f_{*}\left(F^{\prime}-F\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left[\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}+\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}\left(\xi^{+}\right)|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad-\hat{f}\left(\xi^{-}\right)\hat{F}\left(\xi^{+}\right)\overline{\hat{F}}(\xi)-\overline{\hat{f}}\left(\xi^{-}\right)\overline{\hat{F}}\left(\xi^{+}\right)\hat{F}(\xi)\right]d\xi d\sigma.

The proof of this identity is omitted here. By observing the inequality

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\xi^{-})\hat{F}(\xi^{+})\overline{\hat{F}(\xi)}+\overline{\hat{f}(\xi^{-})}\overline{\hat{F}(\xi^{+})}\hat{F}(\xi)\leq|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\left(|\hat{F}(\xi^{+})|^{2}+|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}\right),

we deduce that

\displaystyle\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}+\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}(\xi^{+})|^{2}-\hat{f}(\xi^{-})\hat{F}(\xi^{+})\overline{\hat{F}(\xi)}-\overline{\hat{f}(\xi^{-})}\overline{\hat{F}(\xi^{+})}\hat{F}(\xi)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)\left(|\hat{F}(\xi^{+})|^{2}+|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}\right)\geq\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}.

Together with (
5.5
), one derives

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(k\cdot\sigma)f_{*}\left(F^{\prime}-F\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left[\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}+\hat{f}(0)|\hat{F}\left(\xi^{+}\right)|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad-\hat{f}\left(\xi^{-}\right)\hat{F}\left(\xi^{+}\right)\overline{\hat{F}}(\xi)-\overline{\hat{f}}\left(\xi^{-}\right)\overline{\hat{F}}\left(\xi^{+}\right)\hat{F}(\xi)\right]d\xi d\sigma

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}}|\hat{F}(\xi)|^{2}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\hat{f}(0)-|\hat{f}(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma\right\}d\xi.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.2
is finished.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
r_{0}
be a small positive constant, and let
R>0
be a sufficiently large constant. For any
v_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
|v_{j}|<R
, we define the sets

(5.6)

\displaystyle A_{j}=\left\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}:|v-v_{j}|\leq\frac{r_{0}}{4},\ |v_{j}|<R\right\},\quad B_{j}=\left\{v\in B_{R}(v):|v-v_{j}|\geq r_{0}\right\}.

Then for all
\gamma\in(-3,1]
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b(k\cdot\sigma)(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}\left((F\chi_{A_{j}})^{\prime}-(F\chi_{A_{j}})\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(k\cdot\sigma)\left(\sqrt{f^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f}\right)^{2}\,dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma+\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}^{2},

where
F=\sqrt{f(v)}
,
F^{\prime}=\sqrt{f(v^{\prime})}
,
C_{2}=\min\left\{\left(\frac{r_{0}}{2}\right)^{\gamma},(2\sqrt{2}R)^{\gamma}\right\}^{-1}
, and
\chi_{A_{j}},\chi_{B_{j}}
denote smooth cutoff functions supported on the sets
A_{j}
and
B_{j}
, respectively.

Remark 5.2
.

This lemma is mainly based on Lemma 2 in
[
5
]
. For the reader’s convenience, we provide the proof here.

Proof.

It is evident that the inequality

\displaystyle f_{*}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\geq(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\chi_{A_{j}}^{2}

holds. Furthermore, the relation

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-F\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}=(F^{\prime}(\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-\chi_{A_{j}})+(F^{\prime}-F)\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}\leq 2(F^{\prime})^{2}(\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}+2(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\chi_{A_{j}}^{2}

implies that for any
\gamma\in[0,1]
, we have the lower bound

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(k\cdot\sigma)f_{*}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\min\{1,|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma})\}b(k\cdot\sigma)(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}(F^{\prime}-F)^{2}\chi_{A_{j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\min\{1,|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\}b(k\cdot\sigma)(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}(F^{\prime}\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-F\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}

\displaystyle-b(k\cdot\sigma)(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}(F^{\prime})^{2}(\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-\chi_{A_{j}})^{2},

where we have used the estimate

\displaystyle(\chi_{A_{j}}^{\prime}-\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}\leq\|\nabla\chi_{A_{j}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}|v^{\prime}-v|^{2}=\|\nabla\chi_{A_{j}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}|v-v_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right).

By the definition of the set
A_{j}
, we assume the gradient bound
|\nabla\chi_{A_{j}}|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{1}r_{0}^{-2}
. Consequently, estimating the term
(F^{\prime})^{2}(\chi^{\prime}_{A_{j}}-\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}
yields

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}b(k\cdot\sigma)f^{\prime}(\chi^{\prime}_{A_{j}}-\chi_{A_{j}})^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}dvdv_{*}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}f^{\prime}f_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{2}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin\theta d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{{v}^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}dv^{\prime}dv_{*}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}f^{\prime}f_{*}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|^{2}\cdot 4\cos^{-2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\sin\theta d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{{v}^{\prime}}}f^{\prime}(1+|v^{\prime}|^{2})dv^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dv_{*}=\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

We observe that the term
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}
vanishes as the distance
|v-v_{*}|
tends to 0 for
\gamma\in(0,1]
. To address this singularity, we construct the sets
A_{j}
and
B_{j}
such that they are initially separated by a distance of
3r_{0}/4
. Consequently, even after mollification, their supports remain separated by at least
r_{0}/2
. This separation ensures that if
v_{*}\in B_{j}
and either
v\in A_{j}
or
v^{\prime}\in A_{j}
, we have the bounds

\displaystyle r_{0}/2<|v-v_{*}|\leq\sqrt{2}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|\leq 2\sqrt{2}R.

Let us define the constant
C_{2}
by
C_{2}=\left(\min_{r_{0}/2\leq|z|\leq 2\sqrt{2}R}\{1,|z|^{\gamma}\}\right)^{-1}
.
Since
r_{0}>0
is sufficiently small and
R
is sufficiently large, this constant simplifies to

C_{2}=\min\{(r_{0}/2)^{\gamma},(2\sqrt{2}R)^{\gamma}\}^{-1}\,.

Finally, combining these estimates leads to

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b(k\cdot\sigma)(f\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}\left((F\chi_{A_{j}})^{\prime}-(F\chi_{A_{j}})\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(k\cdot\sigma)\left(\sqrt{f^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f}\right)^{2}\,dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma+\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}^{2}.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.3
is finished.
∎

Based on the above lemmas, we are now in a position to provide the detailed proof of inequality (
5.3
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\chi_{R}
be a smooth cutoff function satisfying
0\leq\chi_{R}\leq 1
with
\chi_{R}\equiv 1
on
B_{R}(v)
and
\supp(\chi_{R})\subset B_{R+1}(v)
. Then for all
\gamma\in(-3,1]
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}(t,x,v)dv+\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f^{n}_{*}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right\}.

Here,
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform with respect to the velocity variable
v
,
C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}
is a positive constant, while
r_{0},R>0
are sufficiently small and large constants, respectively.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
5.1
to the function
f^{n}\chi_{B_{j}}
, where
\chi_{B_{j}}
denotes a smooth cutoff function supported on
B_{j}
as defined in (
5.6
), establishes that for
|\xi|\geq 1
the function
Z_{n}
satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)\leq C_{0}\|f^{n}\chi_{B_{j}}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(0)-|\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma,

where we choose an appropriate
r_{0}
such that for any
|v_{0}|<R
, the condition

\int_{|v-v_{0}|<r_{0}}f^{n}(v)dv\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}(v)}f^{n}(v)dv

holds. Combined with the definition of the set
B_{j}
, this implies the bound
\|f^{n}\chi_{B_{j}}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\leq 2\|f^{n}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}
. Consequently, for
|\xi|\geq 1
, we obtain the inequality

(5.7)

\displaystyle Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)\leq C_{0}\|f^{n}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(0)-|\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma.

Based on the definition of the set
A_{j}
in (
5.6
), the finite covering theorem implies the existence of a finite collection of points
\{v_{j}\}_{j=1}^{J}
such that
B_{R}\subset\cup_{j}^{J}A_{j}
. We then construct a partition of unity
\{\phi_{j}\}^{J}_{j=1}
(for instance, by setting
\phi_{j}=\chi_{A_{j}}/\sum^{J}_{k=1}\chi_{A_{k}}
) satisfying the conditions

\displaystyle\supp\phi_{j}\subset B_{R},\quad 0\leq\phi_{j}\leq 1,\quad\sum^{J}_{j=1}\phi_{j}(v)=1,\ \text{ for }v\in B_{R}.

We decompose the function as
\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R}=\sum^{J}_{j=1}h_{j}
, where
h_{j}(v)=\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R}\phi_{j}
. The linearity of the Fourier transform and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality imply

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})(\xi)|^{2}=\left|\sum^{J}_{j=1}\mathcal{F}h_{j}(\xi)\right|^{2}\leq J\sum^{J}_{j=1}\left|\mathcal{F}h_{j}(\xi)\right|^{2}\leq J\sum^{J}_{j=1}\left|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}})(\xi)\right|^{2},

where we have used the pointwise bound
0\leq h_{j}(v)\leq\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R}\chi_{A_{j}}=\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}}
. Combining this result with Lemma
5.2
yields the estimate

(5.8)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(0)-|\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle J\sum^{J}_{j=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}}\left|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}})\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)\left(\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(0)-|\mathcal{F}({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})(\xi^{-})|\right)d\sigma d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{3}J\sum^{J}_{j=1}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}\left((\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}}){{}^{\prime}}-(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}})\right)^{2}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma.

Furthermore, Lemma
5.3
provides the upper bound

(5.9)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{n}\left(\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\cdot\sigma\right)({f}^{n}\chi_{B_{j}})_{*}\left((\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}}){{}^{\prime}}-(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{A_{j}})\right)^{2}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}_{*}(\sqrt{f^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}-\sqrt{f^{n}})^{2}\,dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma+\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}.

Combining estimates (
5.7
)-(
5.9
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}}\left\{\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}\left(\sqrt{f_{*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}\,dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma+\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\right\}.

Applying the elementary inequality
(\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y})^{2}\leq x\log\frac{x}{y}-x+y
, we bound the first term as

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}\left(\sqrt{f_{*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}\,dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}\log\frac{f^{n}_{*}}{{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma,

where we observe that

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}\log\frac{f^{n}_{*}}{f_{*}^{n^{\prime}}}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}(f^{n}f^{n}_{*}-{f^{n}}^{\prime}f_{*}^{n}{{}^{\prime}})\log f^{n}_{*}dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma.

Consequently, recalling the definition of
e^{n}
in (
5.1
), we arrive at the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}(t,x,v)\,dv+\frac{C_{1}}{r_{0}^{2}}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f^{n}_{*}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right\}.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.4
is finished.
∎

Corollary 5.1
.

If
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle\int_{|\xi|\geq 1}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\chi_{R})|^{2}Z_{n}\left(\frac{1}{|\xi|}\right)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}J^{2}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}\left(r_{0}/2\right)^{\gamma}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}(t,x,v)\,dv+\left(C_{\nu}+\frac{C_{1}}{r^{2}_{0}}\right)\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\right\}.

Here,
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform with respect to the velocity variable
v
, and
C_{0}J^{2}
represents a positive constant. Moreover,
r_{0}
denotes a sufficiently small constant.

Proof.

First, we obtain the inequality (
5.3
) in Lemma
5.4
, then we only need to handle the term
B_{n}f^{n}({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})
. Replacing
S
with
S^{n}
in Lemma
4.1
yields

S^{n}(|z|)\leq C\left(M_{n}(|z|)+|z|M^{\prime}_{n}(|z|)\right).

Moreover, according to the construction of
M_{n}
in Lemma
3.2
, there exists a constant
C_{\nu}
independent of
n
such that
M_{n}(|z|)+|z|M^{\prime}_{n}(|z|)\leq C_{\nu}|z|^{\gamma}
. Then for any
\gamma\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dv\,dv_{*}\,d\sigma\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}(|v-v_{*}|^{2}+1)dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}(1+|v|^{2})dv\right)^{2}.

Substituting the above result into (
5.3
) shows that the conclusion holds.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

We remark that the upper bound established above is restricted to the range
\gamma\in[0,1]
. For the case of soft potentials where
-3<\gamma<0
, the estimate corresponding to (
5.3
) takes the form

\frac{C_{0}(2\pi)^{3}}{\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}(2\sqrt{2}R)^{\gamma}}\left\{\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\,dv\,dv_{*}+\|e^{n}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}+C_{1}\|f^{n}\|^{2}_{L^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\right\}.

When mass concentration couples with the singularity of the soft potential kernel
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}
, the collision integral term
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\,dv\,dv_{*}
may diverge to infinity. We illustrate through Example
9.1
that this case can indeed occur.

5.2.
Convergence of approximated solution
f^{n}

In this subsection, we investigate the convergence of the positive solutions
f^{n}(t,x,v)
to the approximated equation (
3.1
), relying on the uniform estimates established in Lemma
3.5
.

First, by the Averaging velocity lemma (Lemma
2.10
), we conclude that for any bounded sequence
\{\varphi^{n}\}\subset L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
converging to
\varphi
almost everywhere, the sequence of integrals
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})\varphi^{n}\,dv
converges strongly in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
to
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\beta_{\delta}\varphi\,dv
, where
\beta_{\delta}
denotes the weak limit of
\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
. Consequently, combining the convergence properties of
\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})
and
f^{n}
, we deduce that for any
1\leq p<\infty
, the convergence holds

(5.10)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}\varphi^{n}dv\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\varphi dv\quad\text{ in }\ L^{p}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})).

For detailed proofs, we refer the reader to Section 5 of
[
43
]
.

Furthermore, utilizing the uniform bounds (
3.11
)-(
3.12
) on
f^{n}
and the properties of
L^{p}
spaces for
1<p<\infty
, we infer that for any sufficiently large
R>0
, there exists a function
g\in L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))
such that the sequence satisfies

(5.11)

\displaystyle g^{n}=\sqrt{f^{n}}\rightharpoonup g\quad\text{ in }\ L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)).

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, let

(5.12)

\displaystyle W_{\epsilon}=\left\{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x):\int_{B_{R}(v)}g^{2}dv>\epsilon\right\}.

We thus obtain the velocity regularity estimate, which is given by

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to+\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{W_{\epsilon}}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f_{R}^{n}}|^{2}d\xi=0,

where
f_{R}^{n}=f^{n}\chi_{R}
. Here
\chi_{R}(v)
is a smooth cutoff function supported in
B_{3R}(v)
and equal to
1
on
B_{2R}(v)
.

Proof.

On the set
W_{\epsilon}
, the weak lower semicontinuity of the norm, combined with the
L^{1}
convergence of
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}\,dv
to
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f\,dv
, implies

\displaystyle\epsilon<\int_{B_{R}(v)}g^{2}dv\leq\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{B_{R}(v)}(g^{n})^{2}dv=\liminf\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{B_{R}(v)}f^{n}dv=\int_{B_{R}(v)}fdv.

By Egorov’s Theorem (Lemma
2.6
), for any
\delta>0
, there exists a Borel set
U_{\delta}
with measure less than
\delta
such that the uniform convergence

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}(v)}f^{n}\,dv\rightrightarrows\int_{B_{R}(v)}f\ dv

holds on
W_{\epsilon}\setminus U_{\delta}
. Consequently, for sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we obtain the lower bound
\int_{B_{R}(v)}f^{n}\,dv\geq\epsilon/2
on the set
W_{\epsilon}\setminus U_{\delta}
.

Next, we define the functional
H^{n}
and the set
V^{n}_{L}
by

\displaystyle H^{n}(t,x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}(1+|v|^{2}+\log f^{n}(t,x,v))\,dv,\quad V^{n}_{L}=\{(t,x):H^{n}(t,x)>L\}.

By Chebyshev’s inequality, we obtain the measure estimate

\displaystyle\text{meas}(V^{n}_{L})\leq\frac{1}{L}\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}}H^{n}(t,x)\,dx\leq\frac{C}{L}.

Finally, inequality (
5.3
), together with the upper bound on the set
W_{\epsilon}\setminus V^{n}_{L}
and the lower bound on the set
W_{\epsilon}\setminus U_{\delta}
, implies that for any
A\geq 1
and sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{W_{\epsilon}\setminus(U_{\delta}\cup V^{n}_{L})}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}|^{2}Z_{n}(1/A)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{W_{\epsilon}\setminus(U_{\delta}\cup V^{n}_{L})}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}|^{2}Z_{n}(1/|\xi|)d\xi

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}\tilde{C}L\left(C_{\nu}+\frac{C_{1}}{r^{2}_{0}}\right)\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}(1+|v|^{2})dxdv

\displaystyle+\tilde{C}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}(t,x,v)dxdv\leq C_{L,\epsilon,\delta},

where
\tilde{C}=16C_{0}C_{2}J^{2}\pi^{3}
is a constant. Taking the limit superior with respect to
n
leads to the estimate

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{W_{\epsilon}\setminus(U_{\delta}\cup V_{L}^{n})}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f_{R}^{n}}|^{2}\,d\xi\leq\frac{C_{L,\epsilon,\delta}}{Z_{\infty}(1/A)},

where the function
Z_{\infty}
satisfies

\displaystyle Z_{\infty}(a)=2\pi\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{a}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\sin\theta\,d\theta\geq 2\pi\int_{a}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}b_{n}(\cos\theta)\sin\theta\,d\theta.

Consequently, we have
\lim_{a\to 0^{+}}Z_{\infty}(a)=+\infty
. This implies that for any fixed parameters
L,\epsilon,\delta>0
, the vanishing limit

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to\infty}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{W_{\epsilon}\setminus(U_{\delta}\cup V_{L}^{n})}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f_{R}^{n}}|^{2}\,d\xi=0

holds. Since the measure bound
|U_{\delta}\cup V_{L}^{n}|\leq\delta+\frac{C}{L}
holds and the sequence
f^{n}
is uniformly integrable, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\delta\rightarrow 0\\
L\rightarrow+\infty\end{subarray}}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{U_{\delta}\cup V_{L}^{n}}dt\,dx\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{\xi}}\left|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f_{R}^{n}}\right|^{2}\,d\xi=0.

Combining these results, we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to+\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{W_{\epsilon}}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}\sqrt{f_{R}^{n}}|^{2}\,d\xi=0.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.5
.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\{f^{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of approximate solutions satisfying the uniform bounds (
3.11
)-(
3.12
), and let
f
be its weak
L^{1}
-limit. Then,
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.

Proof.

To establish the strong convergence of
f^{n}
, we first recall the weak convergence result

\displaystyle g^{n}=\sqrt{f^{n}}\rightharpoonup g\quad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)),

where the limit function satisfies
g\in L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))
. We then proceed by considering the cases where
g
is either zero or positive.

Case 1. For almost every
(t,x,v)\in(0,T)\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v),\ g(t,x,v)=0
.

The weak convergence of
\sqrt{f^{n}}
implies

(5.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}\sqrt{f^{n}}\,dx\,dv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}g\,dx\,dv=0.

Moreover, the uniform integrability of
f^{n}
(derived from the uniform bounds) ensures that for every
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant
M>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}f^{n}\mathbf{1}_{\{f^{n}\geq M\}}\,dx\,dv<\epsilon/2.

For this fixed
\epsilon
, the limit (
5.13
) guarantees the existence of
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for all
n>N
, we have the bound

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}\sqrt{f^{n}}\,dx\,dv<\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{M}}.

Consequently, for any
\epsilon>0
and
n>N
, the decomposition of the integral yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}f^{n}\,dx\,dv

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}\left(f^{n}\mathbf{1}_{\{f^{n}\geq M\}}+f^{n}\mathbf{1}_{\{f^{n}\leq M\}}\right)\,dx\,dv

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{M}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)}\sqrt{f^{n}}\,dx\,dv+\frac{\epsilon}{2}<\epsilon.

Therefore, given the non-negativity of
f^{n}
, we conclude that
f^{n}
converges strongly to
0
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))
.

Case 2. For almost every
(t,x,v)\in(0,T)\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v),\ g(t,x,v)>0
.

We have obtained a smoothing estimate for the velocity variable in Lemma
5.5
. Next, assume that
\rho_{\delta}=\delta^{-3}\rho\left(\frac{\cdot}{\delta}\right)
is a mollifier in the velocity variable. By Parseval’s identity, one can derive

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(v))}=\|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}})-\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}\ast\rho_{\delta})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})},

where
\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}\ast\rho_{\delta})=\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}})\cdot\mathcal{F}(\rho_{\delta})
.
Given that
\mathcal{F}(\rho_{\delta}(v))=\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}\mathcal{F}\left(\rho\left(\frac{v}{\delta}\right)\right)=\hat{\rho}(\delta\xi)
and
\hat{\rho}(0)=1
, we observe that
\hat{\rho}(\delta\xi)\approx 1
whenever
|\xi|\ll\delta
. Therefore, as
\delta\rightarrow 0_{+}
, for any fixed
A
,
|1-\mathcal{F}(\rho_{\delta})|\rightarrow 0
uniformly for
|\xi|\leq A
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\int_{|\xi|\leq A}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}})|^{2}(1-\mathcal{F}(\rho_{\delta}))^{2}d\xi=0.

From this, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n}\int_{W_{\epsilon}}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\leq A}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}})|^{2}(1-\mathcal{F}(\rho_{\delta}))^{2}d\xi

\displaystyle+\int_{W_{\epsilon}}dt\,dx\int_{|\xi|\geq A}|\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}})|^{2}d\xi.

This implies

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{R}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}=0.

By the truncation method, it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\sqrt{f^{n}}-\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}=0.

According to (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}fdv\quad\text{ in }\ L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}).

Consequently, for almost every
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, the convergence
\int_{B_{R}(v)}f^{n}dv\rightarrow\int_{B_{R}(v)}fdv
holds. Specifically, for almost every
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, the bound
\|\sqrt{f^{n}}(t,x,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(v))}\leq C(t,x)
is satisfied. Due to the reflexivity of the
L^{2}
space, for almost all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, we have the weak convergence

\displaystyle\sqrt{f^{n}}(t,x,\cdot)\rightharpoonup g(t,x,\cdot)\quad\text{ in }L^{2}(B_{R}(v)).

Moreover, for almost all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(v))}\leq\|\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))}\cdot\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(v))}\leq C(t,x),

which implies that for almost all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, the sequence
\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}
is bounded in
L^{2}(B_{R}(v))
. Besides, for any
h\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}(\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta})-\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))}\|\tau_{h}\rho_{\delta}-\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\rightarrow 0,\quad(h\rightarrow 0).

By the M. Riesz-Fréchet-Kolmogorov Theorem (Lemma
2.8
), for almost every
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, the sequence
{\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}}
is relatively compact in
L^{2}(B_{R}(v))
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-g\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}(v))}=0,\quad\text{ a.e. }(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x).

Due to the equi-integrability of
\{f^{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\eta>0
such that for any measurable subset
E\subset W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v)
satisfying
|E|<\eta
,

\int_{E}f^{n}dtdxdv<\frac{\epsilon}{\|\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}}.

Consequently, we obtain

\int_{E}|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}|^{2}dtdxdv\leq\|\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\int_{E}f^{n}dtdxdv<\epsilon.

Furthermore, since

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-g\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq\|\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\left(\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}+\|g\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}\right)\leq C,

the sequence
\left\{\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\right\}_{n\geq 1}
is bounded.
Combining this with the estimate over small sets, we conclude that
\left\{\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\right\}_{n\geq 1}
is equi-integrable in
L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))
. Therefore, by the Vitali Convergence Theorem (Lemma
2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-g\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}=0.

We now establish that
\{\sqrt{f^{n}}\}_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))
. Fix any
\epsilon>0
. By the approximation properties derived earlier, we can choose a fixed
\delta>0
sufficiently small and find an integer
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n>N_{1}
,

\|\sqrt{f^{n}}-\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}<\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

For this fixed
\delta
, since the sequence
\{\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\}_{n\geq 1}
converges strongly, there exists
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
m,n>N_{2}
,

\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-\sqrt{f^{m}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}<\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

Let
N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2}\}
. Then for any
n,m>N
,

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}}-\sqrt{f^{m}}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{f^{n}}-\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}+\|\sqrt{f^{n}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-\sqrt{f^{m}}\ast\rho_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle+\|\sqrt{f^{m}}\ast\rho_{\delta}-\sqrt{f^{m}}\|_{L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))}<\epsilon.

Hence,
\{\sqrt{f^{n}}\}_{n\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{2}(W_{\epsilon}\times B_{R}(v))
.

Letting
\epsilon\to 0
, the results from
Case 1
and
Case 2
jointly imply that
\sqrt{f^{n}}
converges strongly in
L^{2}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))
. Consequently,
f^{n}
converges in measure to some function
\tilde{f}
on
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
. The weak convergence
f^{n}\rightharpoonup f
ensure that
\tilde{f}=f
almost everywhere. Combining this convergence in measure with the equi-integrability of
\{f^{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and the Vitali Convergence Theorem (Lemma
2.2
), we obtain that
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v))
.

On the other hand, the uniform bound (
3.11
) on
f^{n}
establishes tightness at infinity:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}\left(\mathbf{1}_{|x|\geq R}+\mathbf{1}_{|v|\geq R}\right)\,dx\,dv\leq\frac{1}{R^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}\left(|x|^{2}+|v|^{2}\right)\,dx\,dv\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}.

Finally, by combining the local strong convergence with the uniform decay at infinity, we conclude that
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
.
∎

6.
Convergence of the renormalized collision operator
(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}
and
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}

In Section
4
, we show that the operators
(\mathcal{R}_{1})
,
(\mathcal{R}_{2})
, and
(\mathcal{R}_{3})
are well defined in the sense of distributions. To further verify that the strong
L^{1}
-limit
f
of the approximating sequence
f^{n}
is indeed a renormalized solution to the Boltzmann equation (
1.1
), it remains to study the convergence of the renormalized approximate operator
\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f^{n})\tilde{Q}_{n}(f^{n},f^{n})
. This problem reduces to analyzing the convergence, in the distributional sense, of the corresponding terms
(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}
,
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
, and
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
. Here, each term
(\mathcal{R}_{k})^{n}
(for
k=1,2,3
) is obtained from
(\mathcal{R}_{k})
by replacing
f
with the approximating sequence
f^{n}
and the collision kernel
B
with its approximation
B_{n}
. Owing to the length of the argument, we first establish the convergence of
(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}
and
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
in this section.

6.1.
Convergence of the renormalized collision operator
(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}

Given any function
\varphi(t,x,v)\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
with compact support in
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
, the integral satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}\varphi dv=\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left[f^{n}\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f^{n})-\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})\right]f^{n}_{*}S^{n}(|v-v_{*}|)\varphi(v)dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any function
\varphi(t,x,v)\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
with compact support in
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
, we establish the convergence

(6.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}\varphi dxdv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{1})\varphi dxdv.

Proof.

Since
\beta_{\delta}
is a bounded function, the term
f^{n}\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f^{n})-\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})
converges in weak-star topology of
L^{\infty}
. Replacing
B
with
B_{n}
in Lemma
4.1
yields the estimate

\displaystyle S_{n}(|z|)\leq C\left(M_{n}(|z|)+|z|M_{n}^{\prime}(|z|)\right),

where the integral is defined as

\displaystyle S_{n}(|z|)=2\pi\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin\theta\left[\frac{1}{\cos^{3}\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}B_{n}\left(\frac{|z|}{\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)},\cos\theta\right)-B_{n}(|z|,\cos\theta)\right]d\theta.

Using the definition of the approximate collision kernel
B_{n}
in Lemma
3.2
and the expression of the collision kernel
B
in (
1.7
), we find that there exists a constant
C_{\nu}
such that for any
n\geq 1
the uniform bound reads

(6.2)

\displaystyle S_{n}(|z|)\leq C\left(M_{n}(|z|)+|z|M_{n}^{\prime}(|z|)\right)\leq C_{\nu}|z|^{\gamma},\quad\text{with }\gamma=1-\frac{4}{s-1}.

Consequently,
S_{n}(|z|)\in L^{1}_{\loc}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z})
. Furthermore, applying the Lebesgue’s Dominated Convergence Theorem (Lemma
2.4
) yields that for any compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}_{z}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{K}S^{n}(|z|)dz=\int_{K}S(|z|)dz.

Now, for any large enough constant
L
(without loss of generality, assume that
L\geq R+1
), we consider the following two cases.

Case 1.
|v_{*}|\leq L
.
Let
R^{\prime}=R+L
. Then by applying the properties of the convolution, we find that there exists a constant
C(R,L)>0
, such that

\displaystyle\|(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L})\ast_{v}S_{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle=\iint_{B_{R}(v)\times B_{L}(v_{*})}f^{n}_{*}S^{n}(|v-v_{*}|)dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\|S^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R^{\prime}})}\leq C(R,L).

Furthermore, for any
h=(h_{t},h_{x},h_{v})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, with
|h|<1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}\left(\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)-\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}f^{n}-f^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{L}(v))}\|S^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R^{\prime}(v)})}.

Due to the compactness of
f^{n}
, it follows that the right-hand side converges to
0
as
|h|\to 0
.

Case 2.
|v_{*}|\geq L
.
This implies that
|v-v_{*}|\geq L-R
and
|v-v_{*}|^{2}\leq C_{R}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})
. Since
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, we conclude that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}(B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\nu}}{(L-R)^{2-\gamma}}\iint_{|v-v_{*}|\geq L-R}f^{n}_{*}|v-v_{*}|^{2}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\nu,R}}{(L-R)^{2-\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dv_{*}.

Furthermore, for any
h=(h_{t},h_{x},h_{v})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, with
|h|<1
, we have

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}\left(\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)-\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{\nu,R}}{(L-R-1)^{2-\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dxdv_{*}.

By applying the uniform bound (
3.11
) on
f^{n}
, we conclude that the right-hand side converges to zero as
L\to+\infty
.

Finally, combining the results of Case 1 and Case 2, we find that

\displaystyle\|f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}+\|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq C(R,L),

and that for any
h=(h_{t},h_{x},h_{v})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, with
|h|<1
, one has

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}\left(f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)-\left(f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\tau_{h}\left(\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)-\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\geq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle+\|\tau_{h}\left(\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\right)-\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}\rightarrow 0\quad(h\rightarrow 0,L\rightarrow\infty).

Furthermore, for any
K>0
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\|\left(f^{n}1_{|x|\geq K}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\nu,R}}{K}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}_{*}|x|\cdot(1+|v_{*}|)dxdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\nu,R}}{K}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2}+|x|^{2})dxdv_{*}.

Consequently, we apply the M. Riesz–Fréchet–Kolmogorov Theorem (Lemma
2.8
) together with the uniform bound (
3.11
) on
f^{n}
to conclude that
\left\{f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}\right\}_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))
.

We now proceed to establish the convergence
f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}\rightarrow f\ast_{v}S
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))
. First, we observe that the term associated with the truncated velocity domain satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)}\left|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}-\left(f1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S\right|dxdv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f^{n}-f\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\|S\|_{L^{1}(B_{L}(z))}+\sup_{n}\|f^{n}\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})}\left|\int_{B_{L}(z)}(S^{n}(|z|)-S(|z|))dz\right|

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 0\quad(n\rightarrow\infty).

Additionally, for the tail contribution where
|v|>L
the estimate is given by

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)}\left|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|>L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}-\left(f1_{|v|>L}\right)\ast_{v}S\right|dxdv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\nu,R}}{(L-R)^{2-\gamma}}\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left[f^{n}_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})+f_{*}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})\right]dxdv_{*}\rightarrow 0,

as
L\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently, combining these results yields

\displaystyle\|f^{n}\ast_{v}S^{n}-f\ast_{v}S\|_{L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)}\left|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}-\left(f1_{|v|\leq L}\right)\ast_{v}S\right|dxdv

\displaystyle+\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v)}\left|\left(f^{n}1_{|v|>L}\right)\ast_{v}S^{n}-\left(f1_{|v|>L}\right)\ast_{v}S\right|dxdv\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
and
L\to\infty
. This establishes the desired strong convergence.

Furthermore, since
\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(f^{n}\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f^{n})-\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))
is bounded in
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
and satisfies

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(f^{n}\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f^{n})-\beta_{\delta}(f^{n}))\rightarrow f\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f)-\beta_{\delta}(f)\quad\text{ a.e.}\ t,x,v,

we invoke the Product Limit Theorem (Lemma
2.7
) to deduce that
\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}
converges weakly to
(\mathcal{R}_{1})
. Specifically, for any
\varphi\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, with compact support in
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}\varphi dxdv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{1})\varphi dxdv.

Thus, the convergence (
6.1
) holds.
∎

6.2.
Convergence of the renormalized collision operator
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}

Next, we examine the term
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
. Let
\varphi(t,x,v)\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};C^{\alpha}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))
be a function with compact support in
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
. Recalling the definition of
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
, which is given by

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}\varphi\,dv=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})\left(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi\right)\,dv\,dv_{*},

where the operator
\mathcal{T}^{n}
is defined as

\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi(t,x,v)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}(v-v_{*},\sigma)(\varphi^{\prime}-\varphi)\,d\sigma.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any function
\varphi(t,x,v)\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};C^{\alpha}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}))
with compact support in
[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)\times B_{R}(v)
, and assuming
\nu<\alpha<1
, we establish the convergence

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}\varphi dxdv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{2})\varphi dxdv.

Proof.

Substituting
B_{n}
for
B
in Lemma
4.4
, we obtain the analogous estimate

(6.6)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi(t,x,v)|\leq C_{\nu,\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}C{{}^{\alpha}_{c}}(v)}|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma},

where
\nu<\alpha<1
. This implies that
\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi
converges in the sense of distributions. Combining this with the density of
C^{\infty}_{c}
in
L^{1}
, we conclude that for any
g\in L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, we have

(6.7)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi\cdot g\,dxdv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\mathcal{T}\varphi\cdot g\,dxdv

Since
M(|z|)
is continuous on
\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|z|\leq L\right\}
, it follows that whenever
|v-v_{*}|\leq L
, the term
\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi
forms a bounded sequence of functions in
L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times B_{R}(v))
. Moreover, since
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, and
\beta_{\delta}\in C^{2}
is bounded, we deduce that the sequence
\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})
is weakly relatively compact in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v))
. On the other hand, the strong convergence of
f^{n}
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
implies

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})\to f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)\quad\text{a.e.}\ t,x,v,v_{*}.

This pointwise convergence identifies the weak limit. Specifically,
\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})
converges weakly to
f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v))
. Therefore, by the Product Limit Theorem (Lemma
2.7
), one obtains

(6.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left(\frac{f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})}{1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{n}dv}-f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)\right)(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)\mathbf{1}_{\{|v-v_{*}|\leq L\}}dxdvdv_{*}=0.

In view of the convergence result (
6.7
) and the fact that
f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)\in L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times B_{R}(v))
, we have

(6.9)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)((\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)-(\mathcal{T}\varphi))\mathbf{1}_{\{|v-v_{*}|\leq L\}}dxdvdv_{*}=0.

Combining the convergences (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) yields

(6.10)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left\{\frac{f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})}{1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv}(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)-f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)(\mathcal{T}\varphi)\right\}\mathbf{1}_{\{|v-v_{*}|\leq L\}}dxdvdv_{*}=0.

Using the estimates (
4.8
) and (
6.6
), we obtain

(6.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left\{\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)+f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)(\mathcal{T}\varphi)\right\}\mathbf{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq L}dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,\alpha}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left(f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})+f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)\right)|v-v_{*}|^{\alpha+\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{|v-v_{*}|\geq L}dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha,R}}{L^{2-\alpha-\gamma}}\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}(f^{n}_{*}+f_{*})(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dxdv_{*}.

Since
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
and
\nu<\alpha<1
, the condition
2-\alpha-\gamma>0
always holds. Therefore, combining this with the uniform bound for
f^{n}
in (
3.11
), the right-hand side of the above inequality vanishes as
L\rightarrow\infty
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left\{\frac{f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})}{1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv}(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)-f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)(\mathcal{T}\varphi)\right\}dxdvdv_{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left\{\frac{f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})}{1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv}(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)+f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)(\mathcal{T}\varphi)\right\}\mathbf{1}_{\{|v-v_{*}|>L\}}dxdvdv_{*}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}dt\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}\left\{\frac{f^{n}_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f^{n})}{1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv}(\mathcal{T}^{n}\varphi)-f_{*}\beta_{\delta}(f)(\mathcal{T}\varphi)\right\}\mathbf{1}_{\{|v-v_{*}|\leq L\}}dxdvdv_{*}\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
and
L\rightarrow+\infty
. Namely, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}\varphi dxdv=\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{2})\varphi dxdv.

Thus, the convergence (
6.5
) holds.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

For test functions
\varphi\in L^{\infty}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x};W^{1,\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})\right)
, proceeding in a manner similar to (
6.3
), the upper bound in inequality (
6.11
) becomes

\frac{C_{\nu,R}}{L^{1-\gamma}}\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}(f^{n}_{*}+f_{*})(1+|v_{*}|^{2})\mathbf{1}_{\{|v_{*}|>L\}}dxdv_{*}.

Although uniform bounds on the second moment are available in (
3.11
), the lack of estimates for moments higher than the second order prevents us from determining the decay rate of the tail integral in the upper bound. Consequently, the convergence of this term as
L\to\infty
cannot be guaranteed in the critical regime
\gamma=1
.

7.
Convergence of the renormalized collision operator
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}

In Section
6
, we prove the convergence of the renormalized approximate operators
(\mathcal{R}_{1})^{n}
and
(\mathcal{R}_{2})^{n}
. Therefore, the convergence of
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
remains the only unresolved issue. Establishing this convergence will complete the proof that the strong
L^{1}
-limit
f
of the approximating sequence
f^{n}
is a renormalized solution of the Boltzmann equation (
1.1
).
We first recall the definition of
(\mathcal{R}_{3})
, which is given by

\displaystyle(\mathcal{R}_{3})=-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}Bf^{\prime}_{*}\Gamma(f,f^{\prime})dv_{*}d\sigma=-\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}Bf^{\prime}_{*}\left\{\beta_{\delta}(f^{\prime})-\beta_{\delta}(f)-\beta_{\delta}^{\prime}(f)(f^{\prime}-f)\right\}dv_{*}d\sigma.

Since
\beta_{\delta}(s)=s/(1+\delta s)
with
\delta>0
, it follows that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{R}_{3})=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}Bf^{\prime}_{*}\frac{\delta(f^{\prime}-f)^{2}}{(1+\delta f)^{2}(1+\delta f^{\prime})}dv_{*}d\sigma.

For simplicity, we assume that
\delta=1
in this subsection.

Since
f^{n}
converges strongly to
f
in
L^{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
, Fatou’s lemma (Lemma
2.5
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{3})\,dxdv\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}dt\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}\,dxdv.

This corresponds exactly to the convergence result for
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
established by Alexandre and Villani
[
6
]
. This result also implies the existence of a non-negative defect measure. To eliminate the defect measures, we first establish the crucial average time-velocity estimate for
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
.

Lemma 7.1

(Average time-velocity estimate for
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
)
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
. Then, for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
, the term
(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}
satisfies the estimate

(7.1)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}(\mathcal{R}_{3})^{n}dv\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu,R}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}f^{n}dv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}(1+|v_{*}|^{2})dv_{*}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}dv

where
C_{\nu,R}>0
is independent of
x
, and
e^{n}
is defined in (
5.1
).

Proof.

First, the basic inequality
(x-y)\log\frac{x}{y}\geq 4(\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y})^{2}
shows that for almost all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times B_{R}(x)
, we have

(7.2)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}(v-v_{*},\sigma)\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{{f^{n}}{f^{n}_{*}}}\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}(v-v_{*},\sigma)({f^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-{f^{n}}{f^{n}_{*}})\log\frac{{f^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}{{f^{n}}{f^{n}_{*}}}dvdv_{*}d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}dv.

Expanding the squared term on the left-hand side of the above inequality yields

(7.3)

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{{f^{n}}{f^{n}_{*}}}\right)^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}({f^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n})({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})+\frac{1}{4}\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}}+\sqrt{f^{n}}\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f^{n}}\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}+\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}.

Combining this with (
7.2
), we derive that

(7.4)

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f^{n}}\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}+\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}({f^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n})({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}}e^{n}dv,

where we have discarded the non-negative term
\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}}+\sqrt{f^{n}}\right)^{2}\left(\sqrt{{f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{f^{n}_{*}}\right)^{2}
.

For the second term on the left-hand side of the inequality (
7.4
), by performing the change of variables
(v,v_{*})\to(v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*})
we obtain

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}({f^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n})({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma=

\displaystyle-2\iiint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}B_{n}f^{n}({f_{*}^{n}}^{\prime}-f^{n}_{*})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v_{*}}}f^{n}f^{n}_{*}S^{n}(|v-v_{*}|)dvdv_{*}.

Next we address the first term on the left-hand side of inequality (
7.4
). We recall the elementary inequalities

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{{f^{n}}^{\prime}}+\sqrt{f^{n}}\right)^{2}\leq 2(f^{n}+{f^{n}}^{\prime})\leq 2(1+{f^{n}}^{\prime})(1+f^{n})^{2},

together with the identity
